# Credits

Gacha Garage uses recorded music, sound effects, photos and a font by the people below. Thank you! Every file shipped in `public/audio/` and `public/img/` is listed with its source and licence.
Files were edited for the game: trimmed, cut into segments, mixed to mono (sound effects, radio), loudness-normalised, given fades or crossfaded into seamless loops, and re-encoded as MP3. Some sounds are still synthesised in code (`src/core/audio.js`).

## Font

### Alagard

- Author: Hewett Tsoi (Pix3M)
- Source: alagard.ttf, supplied by the project owner (originally published at https://www.dafont.com/alagard.font)
- Licence: listed by the author as free to use (see the source page); supplied for this game by the project owner
- Shipped as: `assets/fonts/alagard.woff2` (converted from TTF to WOFF2, unmodified glyphs); the game's UI,
  speech bubbles, subtitles, PC store and in-world labels. Tiny labels and the flyers use a hand-made 5 x 7
  bitmap font (`src/gfx/font5x7.js`) drawn for this game.

### DotGothic16 and Pixelify Sans

- DotGothic16: Copyright 2020 The DotGothic16 Project Authors (Fontworks), https://github.com/fontworks-fonts/DotGothic16
- Pixelify Sans: Copyright 2021 The Pixelify Sans Project Authors (Stefie Justprince), https://github.com/eifetx/Pixelify-Sans
- Licence: SIL Open Font License 1.1 (https://openfontlicense.org); the licence text ships beside the fonts
- Shipped as: the Fontsource packages `@fontsource/dotgothic16` and `@fontsource/pixelify-sans` (unmodified woff2,
  bundled by Vite); every kana / kanji in the UI and the pixel fallback for any Latin glyph Alagard lacks (the game
  uses pixel fonts only, never a system font).

### Fonts used at bake time (printed matter)

The printed things baked from photographs (`tools/printkit.py`) are set in typefaces from the Google Fonts repository,
all under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (https://openfontlicense.org): Dela Gothic One (artakana), Zen Antique, Zen Kaku
Gothic New, Zen Maru Gothic (Yoshimichi Ohira), Shippori Mincho B1 (FONTDASU), Kaisei Tokumin (Font-Kai), RocknRoll One
(Fontworks), M PLUS 1p (M+ Fonts Project), Yuji Syuku (Kinuta Font Factory), BIZ UDPGothic (Morisawa), Anton (Vernon
Adams), Archivo Black (Omnibus-Type), Barlow Condensed (Jeremy Tribby), Oswald (Vernon Adams), Playfair Display (Claus
Eggers Sørensen). The fonts are only used by the bake tools; the game ships the baked images, not the fonts.

### Pixel fonts used at bake time (posters, flyers, gacha display cards)

The remade posters and display cards (`tools/pixprint.py`, `tools/posters_px.py`, `tools/bake-cards.py`) are set in
pixel typefaces at their native grid, all under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (https://openfontlicense.org), from the
Google Fonts repository: Press Start 2P (CodeMan38), Silkscreen and Silkscreen Bold (Jason Kottke), Jersey 10, 15, 20 and
25, Micro 5, Jacquard 12 and 24 (Sarah Cadigan-Fried); plus the game's own Alagard and 5 x 7 bitmap font (above). Only
the bake tools use them; the game ships the baked images, not the fonts.

## Photos (CC BY 2.0, attribution required)

The pixel-art shop flyers and promo posters (`src/gfx/flyers.js`) are made from real stock photos from Google's Open Images
dataset (https://storage.googleapis.com/openimages/web/index.html), which collects Flickr photos shared under Creative
Commons licences. The photos were resized and re-encoded as JPEG; in the game they are cropped, shrunk to pixel-art
size, colour-graded, posterized and ordered-dithered, and composited with renders of the game's 3D models and text.
Some of them are also the prints on the thrift tees sold on Off-Hobby (`src/market/thrift.js`), shrunk to 12 x 12 pixels
and posterized, and on the big-print tees and photo knits of the Kirakira Closet web shop (`src/cosmetics/catalog.js`,
`src/cosmetics/prints.js`: koi_fish, red_panda, snowy_owl, saturn_rocket, ferris_wheel, lantern_festival, sushi_plate,
sunflowers, shibuya_night, panda, penguins, lane/fuji_morning, lane/shinkansen, lane/shiba_puppy; the thrift list adds
penguin, arctic_fox, wisteria, waterfall, lighthouse, lane/snow_monkeys, lane/kakigori, lane/hydrangea), cropped, shrunk
to 16 x 16 pixels and reduced to six or seven screen-print inks. The photos and their credits are the ones listed below.

### Lane posters (the posters and notices around the lane)

The posters and neighbourhood notices pasted up on the lane's walls, poles, the old tobacco shop's shutter and the park
(`src/world/LanePosters.js`) print these photos (and some of the flyer photos above), picked at random each time you
play. In the game each one is cover-cropped to a few dozen pixels, colour-graded like old print, posterized with an
ordered dither and lettered with the pixel fonts.

### Product label photos (the label studio, `src/products/photos.js`)

The pictures of what is inside a pack (fruit, coffee, tea fields, a bowl of ramen, the cat on the cat food) on the drink
labels, Mikawa-ya's packs and price cards and Ramen Yamada's ticket machine are real photographs from Open Images, cropped
to 256 px or less, and in the game shrunk to label size, reduced to a dozen inks and ordered-dithered into pixel art. The
brands, wordmarks and mascots round them are original drawings.

### Display-card backdrops (the photo or print behind the figures on every gacha display card)

Baked by `tools/bake-art.py` into `img/art/cards/<series>.webp`: the photograph or print cropped and graded like print,
with a real surface photo (ambientCG, CC0, see "Surface textures") along the bottom. Photos and prints already credited
elsewhere in this file (local files) are not listed again.

### Printed matter from real photographs (drink labels, packs, shop posters, menus)

The drink labels and the vending machines' posters (`tools/bake-drinks.py`), Mikawa-ya's packs, tins, model-kit boxes,
posters and calendar, the corner shop's goods, Ramen Yamada's dish pictures, signboard and beer poster (`tools/bake-print.py`)
are laid out round real photographs from Open Images, cropped and graded like offset print (no ink reduction), with
the game's own names and makers set in OFL typefaces (see "Fonts used at bake time"). Photos already credited above
(product label photos, lane posters) are reused without being listed again.

### Surface textures (the texture library)

The surface textures of the town, the garage, the interiors and the props (`src/kit/photoTex.js`) are made by
`tools/make-tex.py` from CC0 scanned materials from Poly Haven (https://polyhaven.com) and ambientCG
(https://ambientcg.com), and one CC BY 2.0 photo from Google Open Images: each colour map is scaled to the game's
texel density (256 texels a metre for the world, more for things seen up close), cleaned and graded by
`tools/pixelart.py` (polish), and shipped as a JPEG in `img/tex/`; the scan's own normal map ships beside it as
`img/tex/<name>_n.jpg` (half the resolution, for the surface's relief).

The voxel face-detail atlas `img/tex/voxdetail.png` (the painted grain on voxel faces and inside the big pixels of the
pixel-art town textures: `src/voxel/materials.js`) is made by `tools/bake-voxdetail.py` from fifteen of these same
CC0 scans (plaster, concrete, plywood, weathered wood, old paper, galvanised steel, canvas, soil, gravel, rust, pine
bark, cardboard, moss, frosted glass): each reduced to a 128-texel tile, its broad light removed and cut into four
hard tones with lone pixels cleaned away.

The foliage sheet `img/foliage.png` (leaf, needle, blossom and grass sprigs on the voxel trees and plants,
`src/voxel/cards.js`) is original hand-pixelled art made for this game (drawn by rule in the earlier
`src/town/leafSheet.js`, baked by `tools/bake-foliage.mjs`).

### HD surface textures (256 px/m: floors, walls, ceilings, counters, roads)

The close-up surfaces of the game (`public/textures/hd/*.webp`, loaded by `src/gfx/hdtex.js`) are high-res pixel art made
by `art/lowpix/world_arch/hd/make_hd.py` from these CC0 photo scans from Poly Haven (https://polyhaven.com): each cut,
laid out and toned at 256 texels a metre (tiles, boards and blocks one by one), painted over, reduced to a small palette
and finished with hand-placed pixel strokes (grout, seams, nail heads, cracks, stains). Sources: `art/lowpix/world_arch/hd/sources.json`.

## 3D models: the people's base body (CC0)

### Universal Base Characters (Standard)

- Author: Quaternius (quaternius.com)
- Source: https://quaternius.com/packs/universalbasecharacters.html (itch.io: https://quaternius.itch.io/universal-base-characters)
- Licence: CC0 1.0 (https://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/)
- Used: Superhero_Male_FullBody and Superhero_Female_FullBody (glTF). Reposed to the game's rest pose, re-rigged to its skeleton, softened, decimated and fitted to every person by tools/people-base.mjs and src/people/base.js; shipped as `src/people/base/data.js`. Their textures are not used (the people are painted as pixel art in code).

## 3D models: world props and furniture (Poly Haven CC0, Sketchfab CC BY / CC0, and a few made for the game)

Converted to the game's pixel-art style with lowpix (`python3 -m lowpix convert --style pixel-hd`), then scaled, re-textured and compressed by tools/lowpix-world.mjs. Shipped in `models/lowpix/world/`.

- AA batteries by Gacha Garage (made for the game, art/lowpix/world/made/batterypack.glb), CC0 1.0. Shipped as `models/lowpix/world/batterypack.glb`

## 3D models: gacha machines, capsules, display stands, vending machine, held items, the PC (lowpix)
<!-- lowpix-machines:begin -->
Modelled for the game as lowpix specs (the owner's modeller; `art/lowpix/machines/_src/*.py` -> `art/lowpix/machines/*.json`,
built and packed by `art/lowpix/machines/make.py`), after real Japanese capsule-toy machines, street drink vending machines,
the ¥100 coin (public-domain currency design) and late-90s home PCs. All names and marks on them are original. CC0 1.0.
Shipped in `models/lowpix/machines/`. Their painted textures are photo-bashed (lowpix `photo` op) from these CC0 Poly Haven
textures (copies in `art/lowpix/photos/`):
- Metal Plate by Rob Tuytel (https://polyhaven.com/a/metal_plate), CC0 1.0
- Wood Table 001 by Dimitrios Savva, Rico Cilliers (https://polyhaven.com/a/wood_table_001), CC0 1.0
- Oak Veneer 01 by Jenelle van Heerden (https://polyhaven.com/a/oak_veneer_01), CC0 1.0
- Rough Linen by colormass, Rico Cilliers (https://polyhaven.com/a/rough_linen), CC0 1.0
- Rusty Metal 02 by Rob Tuytel (https://polyhaven.com/a/rusty_metal_02), CC0 1.0
<!-- lowpix-machines:end -->


## 3D models: animals (lowpix)

- "3d modelling my cat: Fripouille" by guillaume bolis (https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/3d-modelling-my-cat-fripouille-0ab14bf98e754f8d90fe1bf1c84ca66c), CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Reposed, decimated with lowpix, repainted as pixel-art coats (calico and tabby), eyes, eyelids and mouth added; used in `models/lowpix/animals/mikan.glb` and `stray.glb` (Mikan, the stray, the prop cats).

## Gacha card cut-outs (Open Images: photos CC BY 2.0, segmentation masks CC BY 4.0; ambientCG CC0)

The real-photo cut-outs on the gacha display cards (`public/img/print/cards/*.webp`, tools/card7.py) are cut from Google Open
Images photos with the dataset's hand-made segmentation masks (masks: Google LLC, CC BY 4.0) or by colour key, shrunk,
colour-graded and hard-edged. Card grounds also use ambientCG CC0 materials (Paper002, Paper005, Wood049, Wood051, Cardboard001,
Planks012, Tatami001, Tiles032, Concrete034, PavingStones070, Asphalt025A, Gravel022, Ground054, Grass004). Photos:

## Capsule 98 desktop wallpapers (photographs)

Real photographs from Wikimedia Commons, cropped to 4:3 and resized to 1024 x 768 by `tools/wallpapers.py`. Shipped in `img/wallpapers/`.

- Photo: "Sunrise with Mount Fuji - March 2025" by Romain Guy
- Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sunrise_with_Mount_Fuji_-_March_2025.jpg
- Licence: CC0 1.0 (public domain dedication)
- Shipped as: `img/wallpapers/fuji.jpg`

- Source: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Mt_Hiei_with_Cherry_Blossom.JPG
- Licence: CC0 1.0 (public domain dedication)
- Shipped as: `img/wallpapers/sakura.jpg`

- Licence: no known copyright restrictions (public domain)
- Shipped as: `img/wallpapers/atami.jpg` (slide border cropped)

### Summer Clouds

## The lane's posters and notices, the shop's promo posters and flyers; the lane's shop signs

The promo posters and handout flyers (`img/print/promo.webp`) and the lane's posters, notices and photo-calendar pages
(`img/print/lane.webp`) are laid out by `tools/bake-postersets.py` (`tools/posterkit.py`) in OFL pixel fonts only
(Silkscreen, Jersey 10/15/20/25, Micro 5, Press Start 2P, Alagard, DotGothic16, the game's own GG Display and 5 x 7
font), with renders of the game's own toys, the photographs credited above (`img/photos/*.jpg` and the full-size
Open Images originals of the credited lane photos, CC BY 2.0) and ambientCG CC0 paper scans (Paper001-005).
Names, phone numbers and text are the game's own.

The shop signs (`img/print/signs.webp`, `tools/bake-signs.py`: Mikawa-ya, the tobacco shop, the warehouse, Sakura
Heights, Ramen Yamada) are set in the same pixel fonts on ambientCG CC0 surface scans (Planks012, Wood051, Paper004,
SurfaceImperfections003, credited above); the two bowls on Ramen Yamada's board are its Open Images photos credited above.
